# May Goals Progress Ledger

Last updated: 2026-05-31

## Current Goal

Fully accomplish everything in `maygoals.md`, using it as the source of truth and converting the impossible mandate into shipped, verified improvements.

## Baseline Reality

Status: in progress

Evidence:

- Read all 809 lines of `maygoals.md`.
- Initial current directory was not a Git checkout.
- Initial current directory contained only `maygoals.md` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`.
- No local `package.json`, source tree, README, docs, CI config, tests, or project instructions were present in the initial checkout.
- The surviving `pnpm-lock.yaml` matched upstream `main` from `https://github.com/rishabhcli/losaltoshacks`.
- A fresh clone of upstream `main` succeeded at `/tmp/losaltoshacks-clone-KdHYb0`, commit `fcb1f795a26ce2710e970dc0830ef89d40401995`.
- Upstream contains the missing source, README, AGENTS.md, package scripts, docs, test config, e2e tests, AI server, InsForge schema, and MarketPulse source files.
- Source was restored from upstream `main` into the active directory, preserving `maygoals.md` and this ledger.
- Clean Git metadata was restored from the upstream clone. A bad intermediate `.git` copy was moved to `/tmp/losaltoshacks-bad-git-1779863584`.
- Current repository HEAD is `fcb1f795a26ce2710e970dc0830ef89d40401995`.
- Current uncommitted work is scoped to `maygoals.md`, this ledger, the Venture Operating Workspace and Venture Lab slices, overlooked-opportunity atlas work, and their tests/e2e assertions.

Notable command outcomes:

- `git` commands in the initial directory failed because `.git` was absent.
- `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` failed with `ERR_PNPM_NO_LOCKFILE` despite `pnpm-lock.yaml` being present; the missing manifest/source made the checkout unusable as a package.
- `pnpm install --no-frozen-lockfile` failed with `ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS` for `better-sqlite3@11.10.0`; this happened before source restoration and is not a product verification result.
- Upstream package scripts discovered: `dev`, `dev:demo`, `ai:server`, `build`, `lint`, `test`, `type-check`, `schema:verify:masterbuild`, `schema:verify:decisions`, `smoke:decision:browser`, `worker:preflight`, `worker:live`, `mongodb:check`, `mongodb:init`, `preview`.
- After restoration, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` passed and installed 571 packages.
- Baseline `pnpm type-check`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, and `pnpm build` passed.
- New Venture Operating Workspace and Venture Lab unit coverage passed as part of `pnpm test`; latest suite result: 10 passed, 1 skipped, 22 passed tests, 2 skipped tests.
- Focused browser proof passed: `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/app.spec.ts -g "core demo flow"` reported 1 passed and asserted the new workspace, save action, and Venture Lab record in the final-results flow.
- Demo API health passed in demo mode on `http://127.0.0.1:3331/health`; optional live integrations degraded honestly.
- Full Playwright had 27 passed and 3 failed. The failures are live-readiness assertions expecting InsForge ready and worker `missing-llm`, but current backend probe returns timeout/503 so worker state is blocked.
- Direct `pnpm worker:preflight` confirmed the external/live blocker: InsForge returned HTTP 503 for `https://r5em4tn7.us-west.insforge.app/api/database/records/missions?limit=1`, `workerCanStart: false`, and `liveMissionReady: false`.
- `git diff --check` passed.

Immediate blocker:

- Full live-worker proof and any e2e tests that assume InsForge readiness are blocked by the currently unreachable/503 InsForge backend. Demo/local product proof remains available and verified.

Next action:

- Continue advancing the broader impossible backlog now that pilot signal gates can feed activation-cohort onboarding proof and demand-capture evidence without sending, deploying, contacting, spending, or changing billing. The next slice is to add a no-send email-gate experiment dispatch/worklist that turns ready pilot gates into reviewable internal outreach drafts with replay/proof boundaries.

## Implementation Cycle 170: Executable MVP Release Workspace

Status: completed for this slice, with product-build run proof now turning into a release handoff object per venture with verifier report proof, QA proof, operator commands, and a no-deploy release boundary.

Files changed:

- `src/lib/venture-portfolio.ts`
- `src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts`
- `src/pages/VentureLab.tsx`
- `e2e/app.spec.ts`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Added `VentureMvpReleaseWorkspaceStatus` type and `VentureMvpReleaseWorkspace` interface with id, ventureId, title, status (`release-ready | needs-run-proof | needs-qa-proof | blocked`), appName, sourcePath, verifierReportProof, qaProof, chosenRunId/chosenRunState (preferred: promoted, then executed/imported), productBuildCommandId, generatedAppProofStatus, qaStatus, owner, setupCommand, testCommand, buildCommand, browserSmokeCommand, noDeployBoundary, noExternalSideEffectProof, nextActions, evidence, markdown.
- Added `buildVentureMvpReleaseWorkspace(venture)` deriving the workspace from existing generated app proof, QA report, handoff, scaffold, and product-build command run records. Status determination is deterministic and resilient for missing proof.
- Added `buildVentureMvpReleaseWorkspaceList(ventures)` for portfolio-level list.
- `serializeVenturePortfolio` now exports `mvpReleaseWorkspaceList`.
- `filterVenturePortfolio` now indexes `executable mvp release workspace`, `build first product release workspace`, `promoted product build run`, `source path`, `verifier report proof`, `qa proof`, `no-deploy release boundary` plus all workspace fields.
- `summarizeVenturePortfolio` and `VenturePortfolioOperatingSummary` now track `mvpReleaseWorkspaceCount`, `mvpReleaseReadyCount`, `mvpReleaseNeedsRunProofCount`, `mvpReleaseNeedsQaProofCount`, `mvpReleaseBlockedCount`.
- Venture Lab adds `MvpReleaseWorkspacePanel` (aria-label: "Executable MVP release workspaces") showing source path, verifier proof, QA proof, setup/test/build/browser-smoke commands, status badge, no-deploy boundary per workspace, plus a `buildVentureMvpReleaseWorkspaceList` useMemo hook and five new MetricTile summary tiles.
- README updated with a concise paragraph about the workspace.
- Unit test covers builder, status correctness, serialization key, search anchors, summary counters, and a promoted-run path that raises `chosenRunState` to "promoted".
- e2e assertions added for `mvpReleaseWorkspaceList`, `Executable MVP Release Workspace`, `No-Deploy Release Boundary` in the export payload and two search anchor fills.

Verification evidence:

- `pnpm type-check`: passed.
- `pnpm test -- src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts`: passed with 84 tests in venture-portfolio.test.ts (108 total, 2 skipped).
- `pnpm lint`: passed.
- `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/app.spec.ts -g "core demo flow"`: passed.
- `pnpm verify:maygoals`: passed end to end (DB assets, API contracts, typecheck, lint, venture tests, startup check with expected degraded optional integrations, five focused Playwright flows, production build, chunk budget).

## Implementation Cycle 95: Saved View Import Collision Handling

Status: completed for this slice, with live backend blocker unchanged and no external deployment executed.

- `e2e/app.spec.ts`

What shipped:

- Default `Keep both saved views` behavior preserves the existing saved view and renames colliding imports to `<name> (imported copy)`, with numeric suffixes when needed.
- `Replace matching saved views` swaps imported saved views over same-name existing views.
- `Skip matching saved views` ignores colliding imported views while still importing non-conflicting views.
- Imported saved views still pass through the existing validator, metadata stamping, owner-scoped localStorage persistence, and max-view cap.
- The authenticated e2e flow now reimports the same exported portfolio after restoring a saved view and verifies `Release owner no-send (imported copy)` appears.

- Claude was used earlier in this cycle as a no-tools planning worker for collision policy options; implementation chose the explicit keep/replace/skip selector with keep-both default.

- `pnpm startup:check` includes structured server log counts/events in its JSON summary.

## Implementation Cycle 138: Background Refresh Monitoring

Status: completed for this slice, with scheduled/forced refresh runs now exposed through a structured status endpoint, single-flight runtime guardrails, and contract verification.

## Implementation Cycle 139: Background Refresh Readiness Gates

Status: completed for this slice, with live background refresh dependency checks and rate-limit guardrails now exposed before the scheduler or manual trigger can start a real sweep.

Files changed:

- `server/ai-server.mjs`
- `scripts/verify-api-contracts.mjs`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Added background refresh readiness checks for live mode, InsForge base URL presence, InsForge token presence, `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, rate-limit budget, single-flight state, and optional MongoDB vector sync.
- Embedded readiness into `/api/background/refresh/status` and added `GET /api/background/refresh/readiness` for a focused no-secret status view.
- Added configurable search cadence guardrails via `MASTERBUILD_BG_SEARCH_DELAY_MS`, `MASTERBUILD_BG_SEARCH_MIN_DELAY_MS`, and `MASTERBUILD_BG_SEARCH_MAX_QUERIES_PER_RUN`.
- Manual live `POST /api/refresh` now returns `503` with readiness evidence when required live dependencies or safe rate settings are missing, while overlapping live runs still return `409`.
- The non-demo scheduler now checks readiness before scheduling startup/interval refreshes and emits `background.refresh.scheduler-blocked` when live refresh is unsafe.
- Refresh readiness emits structured `background.refresh.readiness` logs with blocker/warning names but no secret values.
- `pnpm api:contracts` now validates the dedicated readiness endpoint, embedded readiness in refresh status, demo-mode live blocking, required readiness check names, rate-limit query/delay fields, and structured readiness log payloads.

- Synthesis failures or unparseable synthesis now finish the background run as failed, mark the mission `error`, and attach retry metadata instead of reporting the whole sweep as completed.

## Implementation Cycle 141: Background Refresh Discovery Idempotency

Status: completed for this slice, with background refresh discovery ingestion now deduping repeated source URLs and degrading insert failures instead of dropping whole batches.

Files changed:

- `server/ai-server.mjs`
- `scripts/verify-api-contracts.mjs`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Added source URL canonicalization for background discoveries, including lowercase hostnames, stripped fragments, trailing-slash cleanup, and removal of common tracking query parameters.
- Added in-run duplicate detection so the same Brave result surfaced by multiple queries is skipped before insert.
- Added recent-source lookback against existing discovery rows, configurable through `MASTERBUILD_BG_DISCOVERY_DEDUPE_LOOKBACK`, so retried refresh runs avoid recently-known source URLs when the database can answer.
- Added batch insert fallback: if a discovery batch insert fails, the server attempts row-level inserts and records per-row failures instead of losing the entire batch.
- Background run snapshots now expose `duplicateSourcesSkipped` and `discoveryInsertFailures` counters.
- Vector sync, agent source counts, and synthesis now operate on inserted discoveries rather than candidates that were skipped or failed during insertion.
- README documents discovery canonicalization, duplicate skipping, recent-source lookback, and row-level insert fallback.
- `pnpm api:contracts` now asserts the public idempotency counters on background refresh run snapshots.

## Implementation Cycle 142: Runtime Operation Evidence Export

Status: completed for this slice, with background refresh monitor/readiness/run evidence now exportable as a durable hashed JSON artifact outside volatile process memory.

Files changed:

- `server/ai-server.mjs`
- `scripts/verify-api-contracts.mjs`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Added `POST /api/background/refresh/export`, which writes an atomic JSON artifact under `MASTERBUILD_RUNTIME_DIR/operation-evidence` or `runtime/operation-evidence` by default.
- Export artifacts include schema version, generation time, service/source metadata, readiness blockers, scheduler settings, current/last/recent refresh run snapshots, totals, and an operator summary.
- Export responses include artifact path, runtime-relative path, filename, byte count, SHA-256 hash, operator summary, and the matching live background refresh snapshot.
- Export writes use a temporary file plus rename so operators do not get partially-written JSON artifacts.
- Structured logging now emits `background.refresh.exported` with path, byte count, hash, last run ID, recent run count, and blocker names.
- `pnpm api:contracts` now runs the AI server with an isolated `MASTERBUILD_RUNTIME_DIR`, exports operation evidence after deterministic demo refresh runs, verifies the artifact file exists under that runtime directory, checks the response byte count and SHA-256 hash against the on-disk file, parses the JSON, validates embedded readiness, and removes the temp runtime directory on success.
- README documents the export endpoint, artifact location, artifact contents, and verifier proof.

## Implementation Cycle 144: Operation Evidence Import Inspection

Status: completed for this slice, with exported runtime evidence artifacts now safely inspectable as import/restore candidates and comparable against current background refresh state.

Files changed:

- `server/ai-server.mjs`
- `scripts/verify-api-contracts.mjs`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Added `POST /api/background/refresh/import/inspect` for validating a saved background refresh operation-evidence artifact before an operator trusts it as restore/import input.
- The endpoint accepts a filename, runtime-relative path, or absolute path, but resolves it only inside the configured operation-evidence runtime directory.
- Import inspection rejects path traversal and unexpected artifact names instead of trusting caller-supplied paths.
- The inspector reads the artifact, computes byte count and SHA-256, verifies an optional expected SHA-256, parses JSON, validates the operation-evidence schema shape, and summarizes the saved operator evidence.
- The response compares saved last-run ID, status, blocking reasons, and recent-run count against the current live background refresh snapshot.
- Structured logging now emits `background.refresh.import_inspected` with validity, hash, and current-state comparison fields.
- `pnpm api:contracts` now exports an artifact, verifies the artifact index, inspects the exported artifact through the import endpoint, checks hash and byte counts, validates saved summary fields, asserts current last-run/status match, and requires the structured import-inspection event.
- README documents the import inspection endpoint and the safe runtime-directory boundary.

## Implementation Cycle 153: Support Burden and Competitive Retaliation Lenses

Status: completed for this slice, with the venture evaluation lens set extended by two operating-reality lenses (support burden and competitive retaliation), propagating support burden into the core posture set while keeping competitive retaliation out of the posture set so consumer or niche-vertical ventures without explicit incumbent language are not falsely blocked.

Files changed:

- `src/lib/venture-workspace.ts`
- `src/lib/venture-workspace.test.ts`
- `src/lib/venture-portfolio.ts`
- `src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts`
- `src/components/research/VentureOperatingWorkspace.tsx`
- `README.md`
- `.codex-maygoals-progress.md`

What shipped:

- Extended `VentureEvaluationLenses` with `supportBurden` and `competitiveRetaliation`, keeping the existing per-lens shape (label, score, confidence, signals, gaps, next action) so the lens set now has twenty-five entries.
- Added `buildPostureRetaliationEvaluationLenses` that derives the new lenses from `supportLoad`, `keyIntegrations`, `dataRequirements`, `customerSegments`, `claims`, `contradictions`, `missingEvidence`, `evidenceConfidence`, channels, pricing/revenue language, retention signals, and corpus language around incumbents, competition, platform clones, paid ads, and commoditization.
- Support burden penalizes concierge/manual/hand-hold language, compliance domains, heavy integrations, high build effort, and unrecorded support load; rewards self-serve language, an explicit support measurement plan, self-serve activation surfaces, and source-backed evidence.
- Competitive retaliation penalizes incumbent/category-leader language, clone/wrapper/copycat language, feature-copy admissions, commoditization risk, paid-ads bidding wars, and platform-built positioning while rewarding lock-in/network/owned-audience/regulatory/brand-trust moats, retention signals, vertical/niche framing, proven pricing, and source-backed evidence; the lens forces `speculative` confidence when no incumbent/competition/clone/commodity/paid-ads language is present so it does not look certain on consumer/niche ventures.
- Saved/imported portfolio records continue to backfill via `deriveFallbackVentureEvaluationLenses`, which automatically returns all twenty-five lenses.
- The Venture Operating Workspace renders the two new lens entries in its accessible `Evaluation Lenses` region (lens count now 25).
- The portfolio posture set conservatively adds only `Support burden` to the core decision-lens set; `Competitive retaliation` is intentionally excluded so a niche consumer venture without explicit incumbent language is not blocked by a speculative retaliation lens.
- README now documents all twenty-five lens slots and the expanded posture calibration set (and explains why competitive retaliation is kept out of the posture set).

Verification evidence:

- `pnpm type-check`: passed.
- `pnpm test -- src/lib/venture-workspace.test.ts src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts`: passed with 11 passed files, 1 skipped file, 86 passed tests, and 2 skipped tests.

- Final-options and manual workspaces now derive seven evaluation lenses from buyer/pain/wedge, pricing, revenue model, acquisition channels, retention metrics, support load, expansion path, integrations, source coverage, MVP scope, build estimate, evidence confidence, and missing evidence.
- Manual theses without pricing or self-serve/expansion proof remain speculative for the relevant lenses instead of overstating certainty.
- Saved/imported portfolio records backfill missing `evaluationLenses` for older JSON.
- Portfolio search indexes lens labels, scores, confidence, signals, gaps, and next actions.
- Founder execution memos now include an `Evaluation Lenses` section and use the weakest lens for the section next action.
- The Venture Operating Workspace now renders an accessible `Evaluation Lenses` region with all seven lenses.
- README documents the evaluation-lens artifact and saved/imported/search/export behavior.
- Claude was used with `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions` and an empty strict MCP config for a read-only implementation plan and a post-implementation audit; the audit found no must-fix issues.

Verification evidence:

- `pnpm test -- src/lib/venture-workspace.test.ts`: initially failed because product-led growth treated a generic signup metric/data-model field as self-serve proof; fixed the heuristic to ignore generic metrics and entity field names, then passed with 11 passed files, 1 skipped file, 85 passed tests, and 2 skipped tests.
- `pnpm test -- src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts`: passed with 11 passed files, 1 skipped file, 85 passed tests, and 2 skipped tests; assertions cover saved persistence/search, legacy backfill, founder memo export, and import/export preservation.
- `pnpm type-check`: passed.
- `pnpm lint`: passed after rerunning outside a transient Playwright `test-results` directory race.
- Focused `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/app.spec.ts -g "core demo flow"`: passed after tightening the duplicated pricing text locator.
- `pnpm verify:maygoals`: passed end to end after evaluation lenses were added.
- Inside that command, `pnpm db:assets:verify` passed with 5 SQL asset files, 6 parsed live-verifier checks, 22 trends, 20 insights, 10 recommendations, 10 sources, 8 demographics, 5 exported seed JSON files, and zero errors.
- Inside that command, `pnpm api:contracts` passed in 11.8s with `background-refresh-import-inspection`, exported artifact `operation-evidence/background-refresh-2026-05-28T10-32-29-299Z.json`, matching last run `e29f2889-d5f8-4d8c-ab07-65a40f94b5cf`, hash match proof, 22 total API checks, 41 structured server log lines, and zero errors.
- Inside that command, `pnpm type-check` passed.
- Inside that command, `pnpm lint` passed.
- Inside that command, `pnpm test -- src/lib/venture-portfolio.test.ts` passed with 11 passed files, 1 skipped file, 85 passed tests, and 2 skipped tests.
- Inside that command, `pnpm startup:check` passed in 15.3s with demo health ready, live worker/InsForge degraded but reported, and 11 structured server log lines.
- Inside that command, `pnpm exec playwright test e2e/app.spec.ts -g "accessibility smoke|sidebar navigation to Market Research works|portfolio import audit|core demo flow"` passed with 4 Chromium tests in 1.1m.
- Inside that command, `pnpm verify:production` passed; it built the app in 8.7s, then checked 37 JS chunks against `500 kB` with largest raw chunk `vendor-charts-w7yhcmvl.js` at `394.17 kB`.

## Global Acceptance Gates

Any completed implementation slice must improve at least one:

- [x] Better opportunity discovery.
- [x] Stronger evidence reasoning.
- [x] More executable artifacts.
- [x] Real experiment launch/preparation.
- [x] Better measurement of reality.
- [x] Better kill/continue decisions.
- [x] More reliable, testable, observable, or recoverable app.
- [x] More inspectable/trustworthy/operable user surface.

Proof types to collect where applicable:

- [x] Type checks.
- [x] Unit/component tests.
- [x] Build.
- [x] Browser verification.
- [x] API health check.
- [x] Data migration proof.
- [x] Screenshot or visual inspection.
- [x] Audit trail.
- [x] Changelog/progress update.
- [x] Clear next task.

## Current Blockers

- Full `maygoals.md` completion is intentionally impossible at global scale; this is not a stopping blocker because the mandate says to keep reducing impossibility through concrete slices.
- Initial checkout source was absent; recoverable through upstream clone.
- Full live-worker proof remains blocked by the external InsForge/backend path returning HTTP 503 during worker preflight; `pnpm startup:check` now proves that this is visible degradation rather than a blocker for deterministic local demo startup.
- External live actions such as paid ads, LinkedIn outreach, real spend, billing changes, real customer contact, and production deployments may require credentials or irreversible user/business decisions later. They are not needed for the first recovery/product slices.

## Cycle 174 — No-Send Email Gate Reply Proof Recorder (2026-05-28)

Evidence:
- Added `VentureNoSendEmailGateReplyProofType` and `VentureNoSendEmailGateReplyProofInput` to model manually reviewed, redacted no-send email-gate replies as proof conversion inputs.
- Added `recordVentureNoSendEmailGateReplyProof(ownerKey, ventureId, input)`, which loads the current derived no-send email gate work item, fails closed unless it is `draft-ready`, requires owner plus redacted reply text, rejects raw email or US phone patterns across proof text, and delegates into the existing customer interview, pricing signal, risk, or activation cohort recorders.
- Customer interview proof is saved with channel `manual no-send email gate reply`; pricing proof writes the same no-send evidence note into `VenturePricingSignalRecord`; risk proof links back to the email-gate work item id; activation proof uses the existing `pilot-signal-gate` source id so the pilot-gate candidate dedupe remains intact while the evidence note still names the no-send email gate.
- The generated evidence note states the source email-gate work item, redacted reply note, consent evidence, no-send boundary, and explicit no-send/no-recipient/no-deploy/no-spend/no-tracking/no-contact/no-billing proof.
- Venture Lab now shows that redacted replies can become interview, pricing, risk, or activation proof only after raw email and phone text is removed.
- README documents the recorder, proof targets, fail-closed states, raw identifier rejection, and external-side-effect boundary.
- Unit coverage proves all four proof conversions, confirms no outreach approval/send side effect is created, verifies portfolio search finds the stored no-send reply proof, rejects unsafe raw email text without mutating storage, and rejects conversion after a high-risk blocker re-blocks the email gate.
- Core Playwright coverage asserts the rendered no-send worklist exposes the redacted-reply proof conversion boundary.

## Cycle 208 - Appeal-Clearance Durability Monitoring (2026-05-31)

Evidence:
- Added a `clearance-stale` appeal status and four optional, backward-compatible metadata fields to `BreachProcessRegressionEscalationAuditAppeal` (`clearanceBaselineReceiptIds`, `reopenedAfterClearanceReceiptIds`, `priorClearanceAppealId`, `staleClearanceAgeDays`); the import guard accepts the new status and validates the optional fields only when present so existing exports still parse.
- On a quorum-cleared appeal the panel now snapshots the current breached resolution ids as the clearance baseline. A later breached receipt landing outside that baseline marks the prior clearance stale, preserves the clearance packet, reopens the audit, and blocks stability again.
- Added a backward-compatibility guard for older imported `quorum-cleared` appeals with no stored baseline: the current breached-resolution set becomes the implicit baseline so legacy packets do not become stale immediately, while new clearances still snapshot and detect later receipts precisely.
- Re-clearance is gated: the lead must record a fresh `clearance-stale` appeal packet (carrying the prior clearance id, baseline, reopened receipt ids, and stale age), then a fresh corrective review recorded after that stale packet, then two independent reviewers record a new quorum-cleared appeal that snapshots the new full baseline (including the reopened receipt).
- `RegressionEscalationAuditPanel` renders the stale state copy (prior clearance timestamp, prior baseline receipts, reopened-after-clearance receipts, stale-clearance age, what is required next, and the re-cleared confirmation), and the appeal history now sorts the closure's appeals newest-first and surfaces clearance baselines, reopened receipts, stale age, and superseded clearance ids.
- Threaded the four new values through `submitRegressionEscalationAuditAppeal`, the inner component prop, and `handleRecordBreachProcessRegressionEscalationAuditAppeal`, which stamps the new fields and status-specific next-action / toast copy. The serializer already spreads appeal records, so the new fields export and re-import unchanged.
- Extended the serializer unit test with a `clearance-stale` appeal fixture and asserted its status, clearance baseline, reopened-after-clearance ids, prior clearance appeal id, and stale-clearance age survive JSON export.
- Extended the Playwright `portfolio import audit` flow: after the existing quorum clearance, a fifth breached reopened-SLA receipt makes the clearance stale (`audit clearance stale` / `audit appeal clearance stale`, reopened receipt id shown, prior `quorum-cleared` packet still visible), the lead records the stale-clearance packet, records fresh corrective proof, then re-clears the appeal quorum; export proves the new resolution + three new artifacts + `"status": "clearance-stale"`, and reimport preview now counts 5 reopen SLA resolutions, 5 audit reviews, and 4 audit appeals.
- README documents the `clearance-stale` status, the new metadata fields, baseline snapshotting, stale detection, the stale-packet + fresh-corrective + two-reviewer re-clearance gate, and the preserved reopened appeal history across export/import — so a prior quorum clearance can never mask a later recurring breach.
- No chunk extraction was needed: the chunk budget passed with `VentureLab-B43tTCt8.js: 499.31 kB` and `venture-portfolio-DUs8YHb6.js: 490.76 kB`.
